7～9 月期実質GDP は年率▲0.1％と 
ほぼ横ばいの結果（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの結果。
4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服 
○ 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率
を下押し。一方、設備投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）は、ほぼ横ばい 
内閣府が本日公表した2026年7～9月期の「四半期別GDP速報（QE）」（ 1次
速報値）では、実質GDPが前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの
結果になった。4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.7％）から減速し、経済
の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服した格好だ（図表）。 
需要項目別にみると、内需の柱である個人消費の減少や、輸出の小幅減少
が成長率の下押し要因になった。一方、設備投資は堅調に拡大し、成長率を
押し上げた。その他では、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイ
ナス要因になったが、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要
因として現れ、両者の成長率に対する影響は概ね打ち消し合っている。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。 
図表 2026 年 7～9 月期 GDP（1 次速報）

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

個人消費は2四半期連続
で減少。根強い節約志向
や、耐久財の特需剥落が
影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と、2四半期連続で減少した（4～6月期は同＋
0.0％から同▲0.3％に下方修正）。足元の消費の弱さの背景には、複合的な
要因があると考えられる。 
品目別の内訳をみると、衣料品などの半耐久財が前期比▲1.4％（4～6月
期：同＋0.4％）と、大幅に減少した。長雨などの天候不順の影響で、衣料
品の消費が弱含んだとみられる。 
食品・エネルギー・各種消耗品などの非耐久財は、前期比▲0.2％（ 4～6月
期：同▲1.9％ ）と 2四半期連続で減少した。物価高を背景とする根強い節約
志向が影響したようだ。今回のインフレ局面が本格化する前の2020年と比較
すると、必需品が多い非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6年半でおよそ1.3
倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上昇が、消費者の体
感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、購入数量抑制につながっている可
能性がある。 
また、耐久財が前期比▲3.5％（4～6月期：同＋3.6％）と大幅減に転じた
ことも、7～9月期の消費を下押しした。これは、自動車とエアコンの「特需」
剥落が原因だ。自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に
廃止されたことを受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていた
が、8月以降は再び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年
4月の省エネ基準引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要
が生じていたが、景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入
った後は一服した模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減
少もエアコンなど季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
サービス消費は前期比＋0.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.1％）とプラスに転じた
が、伸びは緩慢だ。イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、サービスや価格帯、顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
一方、設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と、3四半期ぶりに前期比プラスへ転じた。
4～6月期も同▲0.9％から同▲0.4％と、依然マイナスながらも上方修正され
た。 
設備投資の関連指標では、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財総供給指
数（除く輸送機械）や、第3次産業活動指数におけるソフトウェア業の実質
売上高が改善している。世界的なAIブームを背景とするデータセンター等の
AI関連投資や、人手不足を受けたソフトウェア導入などの省力化投資が影響
したとみられる。 
ただし、7～9月期の変化率には、前期発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特

### Page 3

3

許権の海外譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少）の反動も影響している。足
元の設備投資の伸びは実態よりもやや強い結果とみられ、多少割り引いて評
価する必要があろう。 
住宅投資は前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.6％ ）と 、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスになった。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～9月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上
昇による需要低迷に加えて、地震や台風などによる着工・進捗の遅れが発生
した可能性がある。 
民間在庫は成長率へのプ
ラス寄与が縮小 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、±0.0％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラス幅が縮小した。4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不
足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油
の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響が
一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラス 
公的需要は前期比＋1.8％（4～6月期：同▲0.9％）と、2四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスとなった。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じたことだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変動の寄与度
が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備蓄の減少が
小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公的在庫の減
少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.5％Ptとプラス寄与した。 
一方、その他の公的需要項目では、公共投資が前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：
同▲0.6％）と2四半期連続のマイナスになった。台風等により土木関連工事
の進捗に遅れが発生した可能性がある。また、政府消費は前期比▲0.0％（ 4
～6月期：同＋1.5％）とほぼ横ばいにとどまった。4～6月期は高校授業料・
小学校給食費の無償化開始により高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした
特殊要因が剥落した。 
原油代替調達で輸入が大
幅に増加し、外需はマイ
ナス寄与に反転 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.2％（4～6月期：同＋1.6％）と小幅に減少し
た一方、輸入が同＋2.5％（4～6月期：同▲1.3％）と大幅に増加した。その
結果、実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.6％Pt（4～6月期：同＋
0.6％Pt）と、マイナスに反転した。 
輸入の大幅な増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化に
より4～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホ
ルムズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの
輸入量が確保されている。 
こうした輸入の大幅増加は、7～9月期のGDP成長率に対し▲0.5％Ptのマイ
ナス寄与になった。もっとも、先述したように代替調達の進展に伴って国家
備蓄原油の放出が抑制され、公的在庫変動が＋0.5％Ptのプラス寄与になっ

### Page 4

4

ており、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果は公的在庫変動によりほぼ相殺さ
れた。 
輸出は、米国関税影響で落ち込んだ2025年7～9月期以来、4四半期ぶりの
前期比マイナスになった。内訳をみると、サービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（ 4
～6月期：同＋8.9％ ）と 急減したことが主因だ。前期の4～6月期に特許権の
海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げたが、7～9月期はその反動減が現れたた
めであり（これは先述した7～9月期の設備投資の押し上げ要因でもある）、
一時的な動きと言えよう。なお、インバウンド需要（非居住者家計の国内で
の直接購入）は、前期比▲2.4％（4～6月期＋1.6％）と減少した。日中対立
の継続による中国人訪日客の少なさや、航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の
伸び悩みが影響したとみられる。 
一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（ 4～6月期：同▲0.8％）と堅調に回復した。
旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体・電子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸
出がけん引したようだ。今後はサービス輸出の特殊要因が剥落するなか、財
輸出の拡大傾向が続き、輸出は全体として増加基調をたどると考えられる。 
2026 年度後半の実質GDP
は小幅プラスと予想。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期のプラス成長から減速し、ほぼ
横ばいの結果になった。先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～
2027年3月）の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。
個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろ
う。2026年度全体の実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平
均水準が押し上げられることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％
弱とまずまずの成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、コア消費者物価指数（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前年比＋2％台後半まで
上昇すると予想される（電気・ガス料金支援の再開と、ガソリン補助金縮小
を考慮）。また、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを中心
とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、一時的に消費の抑制要因になろ
う。ただし、2026年12月～2027年2月に政府の電気・ガス料金支援が実施さ
れることで、2027年1～3月期を中心に消費が下支えされる見込みだ。 
年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調査）では、
2026年度の設備投資計画（ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投資額（除く
土地投資額））が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した（6月調査：同＋8.8％ ）。
生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、設備の不足感が
強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、設備投資は今後拡大傾向で推移して
いく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」（特定生産性向上

### Page 5

5

設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、また、投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。 
日銀の10月会合は、物価
目標到達を確認した歴史
的な日に。今後は、12月会
合で追加利上げを行う見
込み 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。事前に報じられていたように、今回会合で
日銀は「消費者物価の基調的な上昇率は2％の「物価安定の目標」と概ね整
合的な水準」との見解を示した。2％の「物価安定の目標」を定めた2013年
1月から数えて、13年9カ月でようやく物価目標に到達した格好だ。これまで
長きにわたり実施してきたデフレ脱却のための特別な金融政策が、ついに終
わりを迎えたという意味で、歴史的な日になったと言える。 
今後は、基調的なインフレ率を2％程度の水準で「定着」させるために、物
価上振れ・下振れのリスクを都度評価して金融政策を調整していくことにな
る。当面は、中東情勢、AI関連需要、為替相場の変動（円安圧力）などを背
景に物価上振れリスクが強い状況が続くと見込まれ、日銀は金融環境への影
響を確認しながら利上げを継続するだろう。 
次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％）は、次回12月17・18日会合と予想する。前
回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて「3カ月に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、
日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の「半年強に1回」から加速していることを印
象付けるものになろう。最終的には、2027年半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％
まで引き上げていくとみている。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

Document 141: 261125_citi_aspeed

### Page 1

• AST2700 transition and pricing. AST2700 represents ASPEED’s most 
important product-cycle driver. Management expects shipments to exceed 
10 million units in 2027 and believes AST2700 could account for more than 
half of its major BMC mix in 2028. Indicative ASP is approximately 50% 
above current AST2600. ASPEED implemented an approximately 10% price 
increase from December 1, 2026, following another increase earlier in the 
year. The latest adjustment is intended to offset higher foundry and other 
supplier costs and applies to products shipped after the effective date. 
Because BMCs represent a very small proportion of total server or rack 
costs, management does not expect the increase to materially affect 
customer purchasing decisions. Market-share gains at hyperscale 
customers and continued migration toward AST2700 should allow blended 
ASP to rise even if unit growth moderates. Supply conditions are expected 
to improve around mid-2027, enabling shipments to align more closely 
with underlying customer demand.

combines ASPEED’s management controller with a Lattice FPGA, adding

programmability, hardware security and a smaller footprint for BMC add-in cards. These higher-function products
should carry ASPs above first-generation devices. The GM outlook depends on product mix and cost pass-through

• Architecture exposure, market turbulence and risks. BMC content varies by system. Management offered rough
planning ratios of approximately one BMC per GPU in certain NVIDIA configurations, one BMC for every three
accelerators in some ASIC platforms, and one BMC per one or two CPUs in conventional servers. More switches,
cooling subsystems and power-management elements could also increase management-controller content even if
the number of compute racks changes. While there is some concern on a solution for IMC  (Infrastructure

Management Controller), ASPEED believes that rack-level control remains fragmented among dedicated servers,
switches, power-rack controllers and software, thus board-level BMC functions cannot be replaced because they
must operate independently when the host processor or operating system fails. Overall, despite rising investor
concerns on customer inventory accumulation and delayed platform deployments, ASPEED's diversification across
GPU, ASIC, CPU and peripheral applications keeps its growth outlook positive.

Aspeed Technology
Valuation
We value Aspeed at NT$20,700 based on a historical mid-cycle multiple (since 2020) of 50x average 2027E-28E EPS, as we
believe it fairly reflects the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics, secular growth trends, and Aspeed’s
strong supply chain position, as well as robust earnings growth amid margin expansions.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the Aspeed stock High Risk based on its volatility over the past year, which we think is

unwarranted given the company's dominance and outlook in the BMC sector. Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts and
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth in servers; 2) slower-than-expected CPU platform migration; 3) peer
competition; and 4) limited progress in product diversification.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

雙鴻(3324)：3Q26 財報不如預期，營運逐季向上 
建議：維持買進

一、雙鴻為散熱解決方案供應商  雙鴻成立於 1998 年 8 月，為國內前二大 NB 散熱模組廠，過去主要生產散熱
器、散熱片、散熱模組、散熱導管、風扇等，現在已轉型為提供客戶完整散熱解
決方案供應商。主要應用於筆記型電腦、桌上型電腦、智慧型手機及伺服器等領
域。3Q26 產品應用領域 Server 81%、PC 14%、VGA/Gaming 4%、及 Others(手
機及車用) 1%。另外 3Q26 水冷業務比重 62%(1H26 約為 55~60%)。

二、3Q26 毛利率 25.65%，低於
預期，3Q26 EPS 11.59 元 
 雙鴻 3Q26 EPS 11.59 元，低於台新預估的 14.13 元，獲利達成率 82%。本業
來看，3Q26 營收 97.87 億元，+12.5%QOQ，+64.3%YOY，低於市場預期之
+15~20%QOQ，七月營收有遞延認列，實際拉貨力道未如預期顯著，3Q26 毛
利率 25.65%，較 2Q26 再衰退 2.77 個百分點，亦低於台新預估的 30%，主要
是報廢品打消，預期 4Q26 毛利率回升，3Q26 EPS 11.59 元。雙鴻 10 月營收
33.72 億元，+17%MOM，+45%YOY，獲利達成率 31%，符合預期，預期 11 月
營收及 12 月營收將優於 10 月，預估 4Q26 營收 110.37 億元，+12.8%QOQ，
+45.6%YOY，預估 4Q26 毛利率 28%，預估 4Q26 EPS 14.79 元。

三、營運逐季成長，氣冷及水冷
兩塊業務均樂觀 
 2025 年雙鴻水冷營收比重 35%，1Q26 提升至 55%，3Q26 提升至 62%，預期
2027 年將提升至 80%，帶動公司業績大幅增長。氣冷部分，2024 年月營收 2-
2.5 億元，2025 年 4～4.5 億元，2026 年平均 6～7 億元，氣冷水冷均樂觀，以
AWS TR3 來看，氣冷及水冷比重至少是 70~80%對比 20~30%，TR4 將為 100%
全水冷，水冷大趨勢不變，VR 9 月起量，VR 營收逐季增，TR3 9 月起量 Compute 
tray 及 Switch tray 及 Rack manifolds 都有相關產品出貨，預計 12 月單月 ASIC
營收比重有機會來到 15~25%，ASIC 水冷產值高於GPU 客戶，其中 Mata>AWS，
2027 年 ASIC 增長可期，公司預期 2027 年 ASIC 營收將超過 GPU。2026 年可
視為 CDU 元年，2027 年營收跳增可期。台新預估 4Q26 營收 110.37 億元，

張智皓 chihhao,chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

虹堡 (5258 TT) 
2027 年營運重回成長

永豐觀點 
目前訂單能見度約一季，較先前有所改善。美洲客戶需求持續看增，預估明年成長
10%-20%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係考量(1) 新客戶訂單將逐漸貢獻營收，並持續精簡費用，
提升管理效率，2027 年獲利成長可期。(2) 主要營收區域歐洲及北美地區訂單狀
況皆轉好。評價上，以 2027 年稅後 EPS 5.04 元計算，目前本益比約 8 倍，明顯
低於過去五年本益比區間 10-17 倍，考量營運將重返成長，故給予 10 倍本益比
(原 11 倍)，維持買進的投資建議，下修目標價至 50 元。 
 近八季營收及 YoY 趨勢圖

### Page 2

2

全球前 15 大 POS 終端機製造業者：虹堡成立於 1993 年總部位於新北市新店區，
主要從事 POS 設備及相關產品之設計、開發與銷售，為台灣電子金融交易終端機
(POS)及 IC 晶片卡讀卡機製造商。主要營業項目為電子金融交易終端機(POS)及相
關產品之設計、開 發、銷售，這類終端機通過國際組織認證後經客戶在全球各地
通過當地收單機構驗證後即可用做當地商家收單使用。產品目前可支援各類型的支
付方式，包含非接觸卡片(contactless card)、晶片卡(chip card)、磁條卡(magnetic 
strip card)及新興的二維條碼及指紋辨識支付等。虹堡產品可概分為電子金融交易
終端機、個人金融應用產品、電子式收銀機及週邊設備等。國內主要競爭對手：振
樺電、樺漢、飛捷、伍豐等。 
記憶體價格飆漲，3Q26 獲利低於預期：在歐洲地區需求略有回穩下，虹堡 3Q26
營收 24.25 億元(+3.3%QoQ，+18.4%YoY)，受到記憶體價格上漲影響，毛利率
31.5%(+3pts QoQ，+4.6pts YoY)，因費用控管得宜，營利率 9.9%(+3.1pts QoQ，
+3.1pts YoY)，稅後淨利 1.5 億元(+25%QoQ，+8.1%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 1.36 元，
低於預期的 1.52 元。 
物料狀況預期轉好，4Q26 持續成長：虹堡 10 月營收 6.83 億元(-27.4%MoM，-
1.8%YoY)，衰退係受原物料價格上漲及法國動亂影響，持累積第四季達成率
27.1%，低於預期，然管理層預期上下半年營收比重 45:55，預估在零組件價格穩
定後，訂單將穩定出貨。研究處預估 4Q26 營收 25.16 億元(+3.4%QoQ，
+8.6%YoY)，考量零組件漲價未能為轉轉嫁給客戶，毛利率 31.8%(+0.3pts QoQ，
-3.5pts YoY)，營利率 10%(+0.1pts QoQ，-2.7pts YoY)，稅後淨利 2.12 億元
(+41.6%QoQ，-27.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 1.92 元。2026 年受記憶體價格上漲，
客戶下單轉為保守，預估 2026 年營收 89.1 億元(+13.3%YoY)，毛利率 30.9%(-
4pts YoY)，營業淨利 6.29 億元(-1%YoY)，稅後淨利 4.15 億元(-10.6%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 為 3.76 元。 
美洲地區客戶將開花結果，2027 年獲利重返成長：展望 2027 年，若以地區區分，
虹堡北美及南美客戶進展順利，許多專案已獲得客戶認證將開始出貨；歐洲地區，
法國新銀行訂單將持續出貨；亞太地區，因日本基期已高，2027 年預估微幅成長。
整體來說， 2027 年營收及獲利將重回成長的一年，然需關注記憶體價格對獲利及
訂單影響。研究部預估 2027 年營收 90.5 億元(+1.6%YoY)，毛利率 31.5%(+0.6pts 
YoY)，營業淨利 8.21 億元(+30.6%YoY)，稅後淨利 5.56 億元(+33.9%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 為 5.04 元。

特殊材料蓄勢待發 
◼ 3Q26 營收大幅成長，產品組合優化推升毛利率創高：3Q26 營收
24.92 億元，+54%QOQ，+141%YOY，毛利率 43.2%，季增 7.2 個
百分點，創歷史新高，主要受惠產品漲價及高毛利產品占比提升，
1) 一般材料中 PP 黏合片營收占比提升，因製程較短、人工成本低
且無須使用銅箔，毛利率較一般 CCL 高；2) FR4 材料中高毛利率
HDI High-Tg產品占比增加，帶動一般材料產品組合優化。特殊材料
PI 產品今年營收成長 70%，毛利率近 70%。單季 EPS 6.0 元。 
◼ 特殊材料與高速產品持續放量，泰國廠及昆山廠擴產支撐 2027 年成
長：騰輝電子-KY 長期聚焦少量多樣、高毛利率利基型特殊材料市
場，憑藉自主樹脂合成及配方開發能力，持續拓展軍工航太、半導
體測試、AI 伺服器及高階散熱應用。產能方面，1) 泰國廠已啟動初
期生產，目前產能 3~5 萬張/月，預計 1Q27 少量供貨，預計增加
10%產能，2Q27 後將逐步放量；2) 至於昆山廠持續擴建並增設上
膠設備，預期提升 PP 黏合片產能 30~40%，以因應 HDI 及高階材
料需求成長，並支撐 2027 年成長 
◼ 維持買進評等：預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 分別為 19.84/44.62 元，維
持買進，目標價 580 元(13x2027EPS)。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓 (02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號         Page 2 of 5 
騰輝電子-KY 產品主要分一般材
料與特殊材料

騰輝電子-KY 主要從事印刷電路板所需之黏合片、銅箔基板及鋁基板生產與銷
售，一般材料產品應用於光電板、汽車板等中低階產品，特殊材料主要為散熱
鋁基板，另外亦有著墨軍工航天等利基型領域，2025 年產品組合：一般材料
41%、特殊材料 53%、其他 6%；2026 年一般材料受惠漲價、缺貨，預估
2026 年產品組合：一般材料 58%、特殊材料 37%、其他 5%。騰輝總部位於
中國蘇州，主要生產基地位亦位於中國蘇州(月產能 80 萬張)、江陰(月產能 20
萬張)、深圳(月產能 10 萬張)與台灣(月產能 10 萬張)，泰國廠(月產能 10 萬張)
則於 2026 年 9 月開始量產。 
Exhibit 3:騰輝電子-KY 產品營收

Sources: Company、Taishin

3Q26 營收大幅成長，產品組合
優化推升毛利率創高 
 騰輝電子-KY 3Q26 營收 24.92 億元，+54%QOQ，+141%YOY，毛利率
43.2%，季增 7.2 個百分點，創歷史新高，主要受惠產品漲價及高毛利產品占
比提升，1) 一般材料中 PP 黏合片營收占比提升，因製程較短、人工成本低且
無須使用銅箔，毛利率較一般 CCL 高；2) FR4 材料中高毛利率 HDI High-Tg
產品占比增加，帶動一般材料產品組合優化。特殊材料方面，PI 產品今年營收
成長 70%，毛利率近 70%。費用方面，受惠營收規模擴大，3Q26 營業費用率
由 2Q26 的 19.2%降至 15.8%，季減 3.4 個百分點，帶動營業利益率由 16.8%
提升至 27.5%，季增 10.7 個百分點，3Q26 單季稅後 EPS 6.0 元。

特殊材料與高速產品持續放量，
泰國廠及昆山廠擴產支撐 2027
年成長 
 騰輝電子-KY 長期聚焦少量多樣、高毛利率利基型特殊材料市場，憑藉自主樹
脂合成及配方開發能力，持續拓展軍工航太、半導體測試、AI 伺服器及高階散
熱應用。產品方面，1) PI 材料：受惠軍工航太需求成長，以及 2 家韓系半導體
廠導入半導體探針板與老化板，PI 2026 年營收成長 70%，毛利率近 70%；2) 
不流膠 PP：隨同業逐步退出利基市場，訂單持續增加，目前營收占比 6%，預
期 2027 年提升至 10%以上，毛利率 60~70%；3) 散熱材料(散熱膜與基板)：
除既有車用照明市場外，亦積極拓展 AI 電源、高功率元件及散熱應用，高階
鋁基板單價可達一般鋁基板 4～5 倍；4) 高速材料：M7、M8 及 M9 等級產品
持續進行客戶認證，結合碳氫及 PTFE 材料技術，切入 GPU 伺服器、網通及
光模組等應用，預期 1Q27 逐步貢獻營收，目前高速材料占比仍低，2027 年預
期翻倍成長。

產能方面，目前整體稼動率仍有提升空間，惟原物料供應限制實際產出(目前
原物料取得率已自 60%提升至 80%)。1) 泰國廠已啟動初期生產，目前產能
3~5 萬張/月，預計 1Q27 少量供貨，預計增加 10%產能，2Q27 後將逐步放
量；2) 至 於 昆 山 廠 持 續 擴 建 並 增 設 上 膠 設 備 ， 預 期 提 升 PP 黏 合 片 產 能
30~40%，以因應 HDI 及高階材料需求成長，並支撐 2027 年成長。

維持買進評等

騰輝電子-KY 受惠漲價、轉單及產品組合優化，帶動營收與毛利率同步提升；
預期 2027 年 PI、不流膠 PP 等產品持續成長，另外騰輝積極切入下一世代 AI
產品等多項利基型領域，若新專案驗證順利，有望進一步推升產品組合。預估
2026/2027 年 EPS 分 別 為 19.84/44.62 元 ， 維 持 買 進 ， 目 標 價 580 元
(13x2027EPS)。

00

기아 (000270/KS) 
라인업 확대의 성과와 주주환원에 주목 
2027 년 영업이익 7.96 조 원 전망 
2027 년 실적은 매출액 124.3 조 원(YoY +1.0%), 영업이익 7.96 조 원(YoY -
5.3%, OPM 6.4%), 지배주주순이익 6.97 조 원(YoY -0.2%)으로 전망한다. 우호적
이었던 환율 효과가 약해지는 가운데, 높아진 원재료 투입가격과 유럽을 중심으로 한 
판매 경쟁도 수익성에 부담으로 남는다. 판매를 지키기 위한 인센티브 확대까지 감안하
면 물량 증가가 그대로 이익 증가로 이어지기는 어렵다. 다만 이미 투입한 신차의 연간 
판매 기여와 미국, 유럽의 라인업 확대가 비용 부담을 일부 상쇄하면서, 하반기에는 전
년 동기 대비 증익으로 돌아설 것으로 본다.

지역별로 갖춘 제품, PBV 로 넓어지는 고객 
2027 년에는 이미 출시한 신차의 판매를 늘려 제품과 설비에 투자한 성과를 매출과 이
익으로 연결하는 일이 중요하다. 미국에서는 텔루라이드 등 주력 SUV 와 확대된 하이
브리드 차종으로 수요에 대응한다. 전기차 전환을 서두르지 않는 고객에게도 선택지를 
제공하며, 이 가운데 현지 생산 차종은 완성차 수입에 따른 관세 부담을 줄이는 데도 도
움이 된다. 유럽에서는 EV2·EV4 등 전기차와 신형 셀토스로 판매 기회를 넓힌다. 기
존 슬로바키아 공장을 활용해 전기차를 공급할 수 있지만, 중국 업체와의 경쟁 속에서 
판촉 부담을 관리하는 과제도 남아 있다.

PBV 는 PV5 에 이어 PV7 을 투입하며 물류 및 운송업체의 용도별 수요에 대응할 계
획이다. 차량 크기와 적재 수요에 맞춘 제품에 판매와 정비망을 더하면 고객과의 거래
를 넓힐 수 있다. 미국 하이브리드의 판매 확대, 유럽 전기차의 안착, PBV 의 고객 확보
는 각각 이익에 기여하는 속도가 다르다. 따라서 판매 증가와 함께 제품 구성의 변화, 
신규 차종의 초기 비용이 수익성에 미치는 영향을 살펴야 한다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지 개시 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 12 만 원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. 당사 12 개월 선행 EPS 에 
최근 1 년 평균 P/E 6.5 배를 적용했다. 연간 감익 부담은 남아 있지만, 지역별 수요에 
대응할 제품과 생산기반은 확대돼 있다. 낮아진 밸류에이션 부담을 감안하면, 이미 갖
춘 라인업의 판매 성과와 총주주환원율 35% 이상의 정책에 주목해야 한다는 판단이
다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
Tech stocks sold off in recent weeks, as the AI build-out’s
demand for resources and debt collided with uncertainty
about future revenues, and concerns about some founders’
judgement. Prices partially rebounded when Jensen
Huang was reinstated as NVIDIA CEO, but the uncertainty

remains. That uncertainty in turn stems from doubts over
the ultimate productivity benefit, and about who will
capture those gains. If AI doesn’t produce the hoped-for
wins, or if end-users reap most of the benefits, the labs’
revenue projections might be overly optimistic. Anthropic’s
document filing ahead of its IPO concentrated attention on
this question, though the scepticism and ‘bubble’ discourse
has been around for a couple of years already.

Nobody adopts a technology unless they are better off
doing so. At least some of the gains must therefore accrue to
the end-user firms and consumers or they will not adopt it.
Plenty of interesting technologies, such as blockchain or the
Metaverse, have not broken out of niches because they were
not solutions to problems many people had. Modern AI is
clearly not in that category, though: businesses and consumers
alike have adopted it rapidly, especially this year. But they will

only keep doing so if the cost makes the switch worthwhile.
The end-user must be better off, and this caps the revenue
capture available to the producer.
Another strand of scepticism about the technology downplays
the productivity benefits, at least those accruing in the next
couple of years. This scepticism is understandable. It takes time
to learn how to use any new technology effectively. A ‘J-curve’
can arise, where productivity initially dips before rising higher

than the pre-adoption level. The economics literature has
highlighted how J-curves can emerge, including for AI.
The learning phase for AI might be expected to be particularly
drawn out because of the so-called ‘jagged frontier’, where the
models are amazing at some tasks such as coding but poor at
others. The rapid evolution of the strongest models over the
past 12 months worsens this issue. Your intuition about what the
models can and should be used for must be continually updated.

In addition, the world is in the ‘Gin Lane’ phase of getting used to a
new product, with all the bad and antisocial consequences – cyber
risk, slop and fraud to name a few – arising ahead of the systems
to manage them. (And if the ‘Gin Lane’ metaphor holds, you might
expect AI ‘Temperance’ movements to emerge and move the
pendulum too far the other way before a balanced is reached.)
‘Double-you’ could well be true (but are we past the ‘J’?)
Luci Ellis

Chief Economist, Westpac Group
So far past Solow
If we grant that there will be a ‘J-curve’ on productivity from AI,
does that mean the benefits are still some years away? People
well recall the famous ‘Solow paradox’ that ‘computers are
everywhere except in the productivity statistics’. Likewise, there
was a multidecade lag between the introduction of electric
power and the productivity boost in manufacturing. We do not
think it is correct to extrapolate from those episodes, though.

There are reasons to expect at least some discernible effects on
economy-wide productivity measures within two years.
It comes down to timing. With AI adoption, many people start
the clock in 2022 with ChatGPT’s release, but that might be
the wrong starting point.
Consider that when Solow made his famous quip in 1987,
most personal computers still used clunky MS-DOS. Windows
3.0 was three years away still. Windows 95, the product that
mainstreamed easy-to-use interfaces, was eight years away.

Macs and laser printers existed and were revolutionising print
publishing, but given their price, did not break out of that niche for
many years. While it pains this long-time Mac user to admit, the
introduction of Windows 95 and dial-up internet was the ‘good
enough’ technology that accelerated growth in productivity.
The lag to that being visible in the data was just a few years.
Productivity growth had already picked up by the late 1990s.
We think that the introduction of LLMs and chat interfaces maps

more closely to the introduction of Windows 95, HTML and the
web, and retail internet connections in the mid-1990s than to
the advent of personal computing more than a decade earlier. On
that basis, the productivity benefits are likely to become evident
relatively quickly – perhaps not this year, but plausibly by 2028.
Like Windows 95 to computing, LLMs and their chat and
coding interfaces have democratised access to machine
learning models. Back in 2018, I expressed scepticism that

“There is a learning curve for any
new technology, but we could be
closer to seeing the economy-wide
productivity benefits than many
people think."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
machine learning and AI would be widely adopted. Along
with its sister discipline ‘data science’, AI at the time was an
exercise in replacing relatively abundant skills – the bachelor-
degree or high-school level skills required of most white-collar

jobs – with the much scarcer PhD-level skills in mathematics,
statistics and computer science needed at the time to build
and tune these models. Replacing abundant skills with scarcer,
more expensive ones never works outside rare niches.
The LLM-with-harness revolution has changed my mind.
It has super-charged the already skilled – who would have
expected Navier–Stokes and the Riemann Hypothesis to both
be cracked in the same year? More importantly, though, it has

democratised access to ‘good enough’ versions of specialist
skills. There is a price to be paid for this advance in the form
of slop, but clearly a benefit as well.
There will be some learning curve still, and a blurring of
boundaries between jobs, just as the Mac and desktop
publishing got more people thinking about fonts and graphic
design, rather than delegate those choices to a specialist. The
end result will be a significant lift in productivity, and not in a

decade’s time but sooner.
This will not, however, necessarily mean model developers
make all the money. Unlike computer operating systems as a
platform for applications, AI models have no ‘moat’: there are
no network economics creating a ‘winner-take-more’ effect,
and no lock-in from an installed base. The interface means
there is little learning curve to switch between models. You
might need to check your output against the output from
the model you were previously using, but that is an issue

with new models from the same lab, too. Many users switch
around models easily and readily.
Waves of enthusiasm for the latest model from one or another
of the main labs, including in the past week, or a new open-
weights lab, demonstrates that the most engaged users of
these tools have little brand loyalty. The main barrier to entry
to this market is the setup costs of training a new model, but
there are already enough players, both at the frontier and the

fast-follower open-weights group, to keep competition intense.
Easy switching and willingness to do so add up to little pricing
power for producers. This is good news for economy-wide
productivity, not so much for long-term profitability and R&D
spend for independent model producers.
The productivity benefits of AI are coming, perhaps sooner
than some think. The result might not be literally ‘double-you’
exponential growth. A period of stronger global productivity

growth on the scale of the late 1990s would still be a much
better outcome for the global and Australian economies than
the past couple of decades.
No change to our RBA view
The October CPI data were a shade above our nowcast
(less than 0.1%pt), and we think there is upside risk to our
forecast for Q4 trimmed mean inflation.
As we highlighted in the preview note, though, we think
Q4 and October specifically were the peak. Nothing about
today’s data change that view. We are still forecasting a

negative print for headline CPI in November because of the
large fall in fuel prices. Trimmed mean is expected to moderate
next month as well. There were some upside surprises in
October in key components, including home-building costs,
which the RBA will be particularly sensitive to. However,
a number of components seen as bellwether indicators of
domestic inflation pressures or persistent inflation (rents,
dining out) actually came in a little below our forecasts.

We therefore do not see any implications for our RBA view
from this data at this early stage. The RBA is likely to remain
nervous about the possibility of entrenched inflation. Against
that, though, the labour market, housing market and real-
economy indicators are all breaking weaker than its November
forecasts. The Monetary Policy Board will be in something of
a holding pattern at next month’s meeting and will want to
confirm if Q4 really was the peak for inflation.

Further analysis from the team will be forthcoming later
today. For now, though, we see nothing to change our view
that the RBA is likely finished hiking rates but will still be
considering another move in coming months. Nor do we see
anything to change our view that the most likely date for the
first interest rate cut is August next year.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: in pursuit of future gain
That said, monthly PCE data suggests consumer momentum
waned at quarter end, with real spending up just 0.1% in
September and unchanged in October despite an acceleration
in nominal personal income growth (from 0.3% to 0.7%).

The Federal Reserve's Beige Book remains more downbeat than
the hard data. "Eight of the twelve Federal Reserve Districts
reported growth in the slight to moderate range, while four
Districts reported no change. Consumer spending was little
changed, as households remained price sensitive and were
increasingly selective in discretionary purchases." Given weak
population growth and uncertainty over momentum outside the
tech sector, employers are " focused on utilizing existing staff

more efficiently”, “with many firms reporting stable head counts
and hiring primarily to replace for attrition”. These findings
suggest that, should the pace of expansion in AI infrastructure
plateau or turn, US GDP could quickly return near trend.
The market’s mood continues to depend not only on the data
to hand but also ongoing threats to Middle East oil supply,
Houthi rebels struck Saudi assets again this week with limited
effect, and expectations for the AI buildout, which this week

was dominated by news around NVIDIA. US trade policy
also remains a threat, the Trump administration this week
announcing plans for a fresh round of tariffs on China, the
European Union and several other major trading partners
to replace previous levies struck down by the Supreme
Court. China’s 20% tariff could double to 40% should trade
negotiations break down. India may also face a 25% tariff if it
fails to reach a trade agreement with the US. Uncertainty is

likely to persist on each front well into 2027.
The main event in Australia this week was the October CPI
report which surprised modestly to the upside, headline
inflation rising 0.5% (4.9%yr). The main drivers were auto
fuel (+6.2%), domestic holiday travel (+4.5%) and new
dwelling costs (+0.8%). Fuelled by some project home
builders reducing discounts, the increase in new dwelling
costs was noteworthy; however, subdued demand should
see discounting return. More broadly, underlying inflation

continued to accelerate. Trimmed mean inflation gained
0.5% in the month and 3.8%yr, a cycle high that breaks the
3.5–3.6%yr range held since May. The six-month annualised
pulse was stronger still at 4.3%yr.
As noted by Westpac Chief Economist Luci Ellis following
the release, October likely marks the peak for inflation, but
the upside surprise in dwellings and other early-quarter
components skews the risks to our Q4 trimmed mean estimate
of 0.9%qtr up. The RBA is unlikely to relax its focus on price

risks until well into the new year.
This week, Australia also received two partial indicators for
investment ahead of next Wednesday’s Q3 GDP report.
Construction activity was in line with Westpac’s expectation,
up 0.9% in Q3 and 5.8% over the year. The detail shows
a clear rotation in activity: growth in infrastructure work
moderating (1.3%yr) as major public projects roll off; while
momentum in residential (9.3%yr) and non-residential
construction (10.2%yr) pick up. Further, a strong pipeline of

projects remains in place, ready to soak up any additional
capacity released by public infrastructure completions.
Private CAPEX subsequently surprised to the upside, up
12.2% in Q3, surpassing Westpac’s top-of-the-market forecast
of 10%. The headline result was driven by another very strong
showing for data centre-related investment, represented
by a 184% surge in machinery and equipment spending in
information media and telecommunications. There are also

signs of a nascent uptrend in investment forming across other
sectors, including utilities, financial and insurance services, and
retail trade. At 16%yr, real (inflation-adjusted) capex plans for
2026-27 are the strongest since the mining investment boom.
Ahead of next Wednesday’s release, our Q3 GDP preview will
be published later today on Westpac IQ.
Offshore this week, the data released corroborated known
themes. Ahead of Thanksgiving, US Q3 GDP growth was

revised up from 3.4% annualised to 4.3% in the second
estimate. Underlying the result was stronger growth in
personal consumption, revised up from 3.8% annualised to
4.2%, and a broad-based upgrade for business investment.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
"Australia’s economy continues to operate
near capacity as rapid growth in technology
investment offsets the winding down of public
infrastructure projects."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Edging closer to neutral
We expect the RBNZ to deliver a final OCR hike for 2026,
lifting the OCR to 3%. A split vote seems on the cards,
especially if financial markets remain as volatile as they
have been in recent weeks. The MPC is likely to remain

resolved to return the OCR to a neutral setting, which they
see in a broad 3-3.5% range, with differences of opinion
centred on the timing of increases rather than the general
direction of interest rates.
The MPC has coalesced into hawkish and dovish factions.
The hawks (Conway, Silk and Gourley) are likely to remain
concerned about the inflation outlook and the still stimulatory
level of the OCR. This is Silk’s final meeting before her
departure from the RBNZ, with a replacement for the new

Assistant Governor Monetary Policy role expected to be
appointed sometime in the new year. The doves (Breman, Gai,
Hansen and Williams) likely still agree that further tightening
is required, but may see scope to move gradually given global
uncertainties, high unemployment (and consequently low
prospects of wage-driven inflation) and the potential for lower
energy prices should Middle Eastern tensions ease. Given the
finely balanced 3:4 hold decision in October, it is plausible some

doves will favour extending the pause, so a vote seems likely.
Even so, we think the case to hike to 3% will win the day. The
MPC has been resolved to lift the OCR to 3% for most of the last
six months, 3% sits at the low end of neutral estimates implied
by the RBNZ’s projections, and the MPC has previously been
unanimous that 3.25% is a reasonable target over time. For most,
a move to 3% should be seen as low risk and uncontroversial.
More interest will be in the RBNZ’s 2027 projections. The

September forecasts implied one further increase to 3.25%
by late 2028. We expect a modest upgrade of perhaps 10-20
bp, bringing the next 25bp increase forward to mid-2027, with
perhaps a 50% probability of a hike in February or March 2027
and a smaller probability of a move to 3.5% after that. This
would see the Q1 2027 average OCR around 3.05%, rising to
around 3.35% in Q2. Such a profile would be a shot over the
bows of the markets to discourage “end of cycle” type trades,

while providing plenty of flexibility to tailor the timing of the
next increase to the data flow.
The RBNZ’s read on the weaker exchange rate and labour
market will also be of especial interest. If it views the weaker
exchange rate as transitory, a more dovish message could
result. Its output gap-based framework tends to downplay
unemployment in driving medium term inflation, but should the
MPC place more weight on elevated unemployment, the RBNZ

may have time to play with before lifting the OCR much further.
We see three alternative scenarios for this meeting. In a
hawkish scenario (about 10% probability), the RBNZ lifts the
OCR to 3% but raises the projected track enough to imply a
greater than 50% chance of 3.75%, most likely if it is more
concerned about the inflation impact of the weaker exchange
rate or a higher neutral estimate. In a dovish scenario (about
20%), the OCR rises to 3% with the profile unchanged, leaving

the next increase some way off, as would occur if the MPC is
confident the cycle is nearly over and inflation will dissipate.
In a mega-dovish scenario (about 10%), the OCR stays at
2.75% with a broadly unchanged profile, likely if the MPC is
very concerned that global shifts (the Australian outlook, AI or
geopolitical troubles) could undermine the recovery.
Finally, the official result of the 2026 General Election has just
been announced. The results showed a swing back towards the

centre-left, but not by enough to allow the centre-left a realistic
path to power. The Centre-right grouping of National, NZ First
and ACT secured 65 seats in a 130-seat parliament. These results
imply the centre-right will need the support of The Opportunity
Party to return to government. The size of the National Party
overhang was reduced by 2 seats reflecting the overturning of the
narrow leads held in Hamilton East (Labour won by 173 votes)
and Wairarapa (Labour won by 81 votes). These narrow margins

raise the prospect of further delays in the negotiating process as
recounts may be requests. Such recounts don’t seem likely to shift
the results based on historical experience.
A tough period of negotiations between the centre-right
parties and TOP lies ahead. TOP has indicated it will reach out
to the National Party to begin discussions. However, as the
agreement of all three parties on the right will be required, the
likelihood is it will be the New Year before a new agreement

is forged. Uncertainty may plague markets for a while even
though the initial take of the market was for a small rise in the
NZD and short-term interest rates.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Q3 Inventories (%qtr)
Nov 30, Last: –0.2, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: -0.3, Range: -1.6 to 0.5
Private non-farm inventories fell 0.2% in Q2, driven by a
rundown in mining inventories, unwinding a build-up from Q1
which reflected weather-related port closures. Consumer-

facing industries recorded inventory gains, including retail
trade, wholesale trade, and accommodation and food services.
For Q3, we expect private non-farm inventories to decline
0.5%, detracting 0.1ppt from GDP for the quarter. Non-mining
businesses likely drew down inventories to meet demand
amid subdued imports of consumer and intermediate goods.
Consumer goods imports increased by just 1.5%qtr, while
we estimate imports of intermediate goods, including fuels,

declined by almost 7%qtr, suggesting businesses relied more
heavily on existing inventories. This is expected to be partly
offset by a modest rebuild in mining inventories.
AUS: Oct Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Mkt f/c: 0.3, Range: -0.2 to 0.7
The monthly household spending indicator rose 0.6%mth in
September, following a slight 0.1%mth contraction in August,
annual growth lifting to 6.8%yr.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker showed a solid rise in the

October month, centred on a significant price-driven increase
in fuel. New vehicle sales (which are not covered by the tracker
but are in scope for the spending indicator) look to have posted
a rise as well. Overall we expect the ABS indicator to show a
0.5% rise in the October month.
AUS: Oct Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Nov 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth was slightly firmer than expected

at 0.6% in September, leaving credit growth running at 8.4%yr.
Housing credit growth is slowing unevenly – concentrated on
the investor segment, with the tax changes for investment
properties announced in the May budget a clear driver. While
the owner-occupier segment has been relatively resilient to
date, some broadening of the slowdown is likely over the period
ahead. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of strength.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift of 0.5%. We expect to

continue seeing weakness in investor credit, and some hints of
softening owner-occupier and business credit over the coming
months.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Q3 Company Profits (%qtr)
Nov 30, Last: 1.8, Westpac f/c: 1.6 
Market f/c: 1.4, Range: 0.0 to 4.0
Headline company profits grew 1.8% in the June quarter. Mining 
profits rebounded 6.8%qtr, partially recovering the 9.4%qtr 
decline recorded in Q1. Outside mining, non-mining profits 
declined 1.1%qtr but remained a solid 7.7% higher over the year.
We expect company profits to grow 1.6%qtr in Q3. Mining profits 
are likely to recover strongly, supported by the large increase 
in mining-related export values, particularly LNG. By contrast, 
non-mining profit growth is expected to improve modestly as the 
Middle East-related fuel price shock fades and demand recoverng 
over the quarter. This is likely to be partly offset by higher award 
wages, which weighed on margins in consumer-facing sectors 
with relatively high award-wage coverage.
AUS: Nov Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Dec 1, Last: –1.2, Westpac f/c: –1.8
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – 
fell 1.2% in October following a 1.3% decline in September 
and 1.4% falls in August and July. Annual growth has turned 
negative, running at –2.8%yr with prices down 6.7% from 
their peak in March. Brisbane and Perth led the declines in 
the October month but the correction to date remains more 
pronounced in Sydney and Melbourne.
The November update is set to be significantly weaker with 
another RBA rate rise in the mix and the daily measure 
(covering the five major capital cities rather than all of 
Australia) tracking a 2% decline vs 1.5% in October. The detail 
points to large monthly falls in Brisbane (–3%), Perth (–2.5%) 
and Sydney (–2.1%). We expect the national measure to show 
a 1.8% decline, a touch milder. Downward revisions, which 
have been pronounced this year, are also a risk.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: 5.1, Westpac f/c: –10.0 
Market f/c: -3.6, Range: -10.0 to 0.0
Approvals rose 5.1% in September but with the detail 
suggesting the underlying cycle is starting to turn down. 
The headline came from a big rise in the volatile private units 
component (+16.6%mth) which was partially offset by a 
sizeable decline in private detached houses (–5.3%mth).

We expect the October update to show a much clearer 
weakening. While it does not map tidily to monthly approvals, 
the HIA measure of new home sales has weakened 
dramatically over the last six months, dropping ~40% 
between April and October. With unit approvals coming off a 
lumpy gain, both units and detached house approvals could 
record large monthly declines. On balance we expect total 
approvals to drop 10% but it would not take much to get a 
more significant double-digit fall.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Q3 Net Exports (ppt cont’n to GDP)
Dec 1, Last: 0.1, Westpac f/c: –0.8
Market f/c: -0.3, Range: -0.8 to 0.2
Net exports look to have detracted from GDP growth in Q3;
we expect a contribution of –0.8ppts.
Another surge in automatic data processing (ADP) equipment

imports is going to be the main driver of the detraction. The
$11.1bn increase in these ADP equipment imports during Q3
will be largely volume-driven, similar to Q1, which also saw
net exports detract significantly from GDP.
While there have been some offsetting factors in the nominal
trade detail – strong LNG exports, a pull-back in fuel imports
– these look to have been largely price-driven, and hence will
have a smaller impact on net export volumes.

AUS: Q3 GDP (%qtr)
Dec 2, Last: 0.4, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.3 to 0.7
We expect the Q3 2026 National Accounts to show that
Australia’s economy expanded 0.5% (2.3%yr). Labour productivity
should record its strongest year-ended pace in almost a decade.
Despite significant headwinds, including higher fuel prices
and interest rates, the economy remains resilient. This is
partly due to a significant tailwind coming from a business
investment surge centred on data centres, renewables and the

infrastructure required to roll-out the AI revolution. Growth in
Q3 should also be supported by gains in housing construction,
household consumption and public demand.
For more detail, see our full preview.
AUS: Q3 Current Account Balance ($bn)
Dec 1, Last: –27.2, Westpac f/c: –25.5
Market f/c: -25.0, Range: -31.0 to -24.0
Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter with several competing
forces. Goods exports increased strongly, especially LNG
exports, although this was mostly price-led. On the import

side, there was another surge in ADP equipment imports –
capturing the investment upswing in data centres – although
this partly looks to be offset by a pull-back in fuel imports.
Services trade will likely be more of a drag – falling
international student commencements may hamper education
exports, while outbound tourism spending is set to rebound
following the normalisation of international flights at the onset
of the Middle East conflict.
All up, we have pencilled in a narrowing of the current account

deficit to $25.5bn.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Nov ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Nov 30, Last: 52.5
Firms remained optimistic in the October survey of business
confidence. Most notably, the resurgence in fuel prices over
September and October didn’t appear to faze them in the way
that the initial oil price shock did, suggesting that businesses

are simply learning to live with higher costs. The cost and
pricing measures of the survey will be of particular interest
ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December policy statement, given the
policy committee’s seemingly diverging views on the extent of
second-round and indirect inflation pressures.
NZ: Oct Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Nov 30, Last: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has shown a gradual acceleration in jobs growth
in recent months, after a flat patch through the first half of

this year. The weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ show
a further pickup over October, though this is most likely
related to the temporary hiring of election workers. While this
presents some upside risk to our estimate of a 0.3% increase
for the month, we suspect that this effect will show up more
fully in the November figures.
AUS: Oct Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Dec 3, Last: 2.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0
Market f/c: 1.5, Range: 1.0 to 2.7
Goods trade surprised materially to the upside in September,

recording a surplus of $2.8bn. As has been the case for some,
ongoing volatility in trade of non-monetary gold – both on the
import and export side – has contributed to significant swings
in the headline goods trade balance.
Importantly, Sep’s surplus was despite the back-to-back mega
print for automatic data processing equipment imports – which
capture the investment upswing related to data centres.
For Oct, we have pencilled in a surplus of $1.0bn. Port data

suggests a lift in volumes for some resource exports and
commodity prices have been steadier in AUD terms, but
volatility in gold and ADP equipment imports will likely
continue to dominate.

### Page 10

Nonfarm payrolls surprised to the upside in October with a 
165k increase in the month and 84k jobs added to August and 
September. As a result, the 3-month average lifted to 137k, 
ahead of estimates consistent with balance between labour 
demand and supply.
November is likely to see a more modest gain and the prior two 
months are susceptible to a downward revision. Still, a gain 
of around 100k, expected by Westpac and the market, is best 
characterised as robust if not strong given current population 
growth. Ongoing resilience in labour demand is likely to see a 
higher level of participation in coming months, which is behind 
our unemployment rate forecast of 4.2%.
NZ: Oct Building Consents (%mth)
Dec 2, Last: -7.9, Westpac f/c: -2.0
Dwelling consent issuance remains firm, with just over 41,000 
new dwellings consented in the year to September. However, 
it looks like a peak may have been reached, with the number of 
medium-density developments easing back in recent months. 
We expect that pattern will have continued in October and 
are forecasting a 2% fall.  In the non-residential space, we’re 
continuing to see softness in the number of retail and office 
developments, but greater resilience in the industrial and 
storage segments.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -46.14 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Công nghệ, Bảo hiểm và Logistics là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,290 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và ACB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -864 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -10,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -22,338 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì bán ròng -842 hợp 
đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,945 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần điều chỉnh với 4 phiên giảm điểm liên tiếp, VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.700 điểm trong bối cảnh thanh khoản tiếp tục duy trì ở mức thấp và nhà 
đầu tư nước ngoài đẩy mạnh bán ròng, tập trung ở nhóm ngân hàng, Vingroup và FPT. 
Hiệu ứng tích cực từ các động thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản của NHNN qua nghiệp vụ mua vào 
ngoại tệ nhanh chóng hạ nhiệt, khi các phiên chào mua của NHNN đều diễn ra không 
thành công do tỷ giá bật tăng trở lại. Bên ngoài, TTCK toàn cầu tiếp tục chịu áp lực khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu AI biến động mạnh và kết quả kinh doanh tích cực của Nvidia không đủ 
xoa dịu lo ngại về đầu tư vào AI, cùng với kỳ vọng Fed nâng lãi suất trong tháng 12, gây 
thêm sức ép lên dòng vốn vào các thị trường mới nổi. Điểm tích cực là lực cầu bắt đáy 
xuất hiện trong phiên cuối tuần giúp chỉ số thu hẹp đà giảm và đóng cửa ở mức cao nhất 
phiên.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Áp lực lợi nhuận trong chu kỳ mở rộng công suất 
19/11/2026

KQKD 3Q2026 suy giảm do khấu hao 
và biến động tỷ giá

Lượt khách ACV dự kiến tăng chậm 
hơn toàn ngành do tác động từ Phú 
Quốc và Gia Bình

Long Thành dự kiến khai thác từ cuối 
2026, tạo áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
giai đoạn đầu

Duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá 
mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu

3Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,066 tỷ VND (-6% yoy) và LNST công ty mẹ 
đạt 2,206 tỷ VND (-14% yoy). Lợi nhuận suy giảm chủ yếu do (1) khấu hao tăng từ 
các nhà ga mới và (2) ghi nhận lỗ tỷ giá 168 tỷ VND trong kỳ, trái ngược với khoản lãi 
tỷ giá cùng kỳ 2025. Lũy kế 9M2026, doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ lần lượt đạt 
19,226/7,435 tỷ VND (+0%/+2% yoy).

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ thống ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân 7%/5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc 
không còn thuộc hệ thống ACV từ đầu 2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội 
Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang sân bay Gia Bình khi đi vào khai thác. Mức độ dịch 
chuyển trước năm 2030 dự kiến còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện 
sân bay và hạ tầng giao thông kết nối.

Cảng HKQT Long Thành giai đoạn 1 dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 12/2026 
và bắt đầu tiếp nhận các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài từ Tân Sơn Nhất vào ngày 
28/03/2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV. Tuy nhiên, tốc độ 
chuyển dịch có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do hạ tầng kết nối chưa hoàn thiện, trong khi 
chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng dự kiến gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận của ACV giai 
đoạn 2027–2028.

Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với ACV với giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng 
giá 4.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 18/11/2026.

Quan điểm kỹ thuật 
Xu hướng & Hành động

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành nến giảm điểm thân dài, thể hiện áp lực phân phối đang có 
phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, sau lần thứ 3 nỗ lực vượt cản 1.750 bất thành, chỉ số đã quay 
đầu lao dốc và xuyên thủng kênh hỗ trợ trung hạn, đồng thời phá vỡ cạnh dưới của một 
mẫu hình tam giác nhỏ, kết thúc nhịp hồi phục tích lũy ngắn hạn. Mặc dù những tín hiệu 
này đều mang tính tiêu cực, tuy nhiên khi đặt trong bức tranh rộng hơn, những chuyển 
biến này chưa gây ra rủi ro quá lớn đến xu hướng đi ngang chủ đạo và chúng tôi kỳ vọng 
VNINDEX sẽ xuất hiện các nhịp hồi phục trở lại, ngay sau khi chớm xuyên thủng các vùng 
đáy ngắn/trung hạn. Yếu tố cần chú ý giai đoạn này là sự phân hóa rõ nét với cơ hội tìm 
kiếm lợi nhuận chỉ tập trung ở nhóm có đủ 2 yếu tố: nền tảng cơ bản tốt và đã về lại các 
vùng hỗ trợ mạnh trên khung lớn.  
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1750 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1670 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích

Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm 
- Dư địa lấy thêm thị phần từ các cửa hàng nhỏ lẻ vẫn còn tương đối rộng, FRT đặt mục tiêu 
chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại 
- Tiềm năng dài hạn từ mảng tiêm chủng tương đối tích cực

MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and

market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.

Investment Implications
With China’s growth model still favoring
exports over domestic consumption,
resource protectionism and an
emphasis on domestic investment not
just in the U.S. but around the globe are

likely to continue to be themes in 2027.
We remain overweight the U.S., as its
well-diversified services-led economy
should be relatively less exposed to
China’s manufactured goods exports.
Still, the impact of competition from
China on U.S. technology supremacy is
an ongoing key risk to watch.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Chicago Fed National Activity Index is a monthly index designed to gauge overall economic activity and related inflationary pressure.
Consumer Price Index is a measure of the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services. Indexes are available for the
U.S. and various geographic areas.
National Federation of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index is a monthly composite economic indicator that measures the health, confidence, and expectations of small

investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THE RISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas
